# Local Run conformance

Generated by `cargo run -p rbx_sim --example conformance -- --write docs/research/local-run/CONFORMANCE.md`. Roblox is the golden recorded from a real server (`crates/rbx_sim/conformance/goldens/`), rbx_sim is this build. Roblox's value is the median over the recorded passes. A number passes when the delta is inside the test's tolerance (`rbx_sim::conformance::TESTS`) or Roblox's own spread across passes, whichever is wider; text must match the common value or one a pass printed. `ok (pass N)` means the median check failed but the value is within tolerance of recorded pass N: the real server is not deterministic (its first passes often take extra substeps on a test's first frames), and rbx_sim models the clean passes.

Known gaps:

- **BallSocket**: Matches at 20 frames; by 40 frames the 3D swing has drifted apart (up to 1.3 studs in X and Y). Joints are now soft (a 60 Hz spring: the arm stretches like Roblox's, 4.01 against 4.046 at the bottom), but the period still differs a little and the difference grows with time; no single spring stiffness fits both this swing and HingeFree.
- **SleepPile.pile_all_still_from**: the three-layer pile is still in 32 frames on Roblox; ours in 60-75, its blocks freezing one by one as each drops under the sleep speed, where Roblox's whole pile stops at once
- **SleepPile.pile_moving_60**: see pile_all_still_from: a few of ours still settle at frame 60
- **BoxStack.top_dy**: Roblox's five-high stack never sleeps: a box in it keeps jittering at 0.4 stud/s and the top sinks 0.0005 stud a frame for seconds; ours goes still at the shared sleep speed (0.6 stud/s, from SleepPile's sliders and pile) and keeps the first frames' settle
- **HumanoidJump**: JumpPower jumps match; a JumpHeight jump of 15 studs (UseJumpPower false) launches at 74.27 against 75.09 and peaks 0.2 stud low. Giving fast take-offs one substep of gravity back (as the 1st-frame loss suggests: 2 substeps at 76.7 stud/s, 3 below) makes vy1 75.09 but then peaks 0.13 high and lands a frame late: the live per-frame rise runs ~0.3 stud/s under ours in every jump (walk 0.863 vs 0.868 on frame 1) while vy1 reads the same, which looks like how Roblox integrates position inside a frame rather than the take-off speed. Not changed for one row.
- **HumanoidSlope**: 30 degree slopes match. On 60 degrees ours walks up and off the top at the slope's speed, where Roblox stutters at the top edge and launches higher (12.8 against 8.6 rise at 90 frames); with MaxSlopeAngle 45 Roblox slides back down a too-steep slope (v -3.55) and ours creeps up it. Simply refusing to walk up a too-steep floor sends ours sliding off the wedge's far side (tried in M10); the wall-and-edge behaviour of the floor sensor on steep wedges isn't modelled.
- **HumanoidPlatform**: A rider on an unanchored raft carried by LinearVelocity matches, and so does the horizontal carry on an anchored lift moved by CFrame every beat; but on that lift ours ends 0.9 stud lower than Roblox's passenger (-3.17 against -2.27): how Roblox's floor sensor keeps a character on a part teleported by CFrame isn't pinned down.
- **ContactSink**: Single blocks of any density and stacks of equal blocks sink like Roblox's within 0.01 stud; a dense block on a light one sinks the light one 0.015 stud in its first second where Roblox's sinks 0.031 (and 0.098 against 0.082 after two). Roblox's contact stiffness depends on the mass ratio in a way one spring doesn't cover.
- **TerrainRaycast**: Distances, both ball height grids and the voxels match. The three ball rays land exactly on a mesh vertex and the triangle that wins there differs (Roblox's own normal at ball_top changed between two origins). ignore_water hits Rock: Roblox's mesher moves Rock and Ice vertices by a pseudo-random offset that depends on where they are (its slides call it a per-material 'shift'): the same probe recorded at another origin gives other heights, so these rows change whenever the test list (and so each test's origin) changes, and the hash is not known.
- **TerrainPhysics**: Grass sliding, drops and bounces match. A ball made half into the ground falls through it as in Roblox, but Roblox's is pushed down by the top surface first and stopped from inside by the slab's underside (`_ball`), where ours falls on. The roller, the Ice block's slide and its sideways drift run over Rock and Ice surfaces: Roblox's mesher moves Rock and Ice vertices by a pseudo-random offset that depends on where they are (its slides call it a per-material 'shift'): the same probe recorded at another origin gives other heights, so these rows change whenever the test list (and so each test's origin) changes, and the hash is not known.
- **TerrainHumanoid**: Standing, FloorMaterial and the flat walk match. The wedges are Rock: Roblox's mesher moves Rock and Ice vertices by a pseudo-random offset that depends on where they are (its slides call it a per-material 'shift'): the same probe recorded at another origin gives other heights, so these rows change whenever the test list (and so each test's origin) changes, and the hash is not known.
- **Buoyancy**: Depths, dips, bobbing and terminal speeds match. `*_settled` is the first frame a body's speed drops under 0.05, which lands on a turning point of the bob: Roblox's small bobs die out later than ours (d03, wood) and the plank's sooner.
- **TerrainProfile**: The voxels match. The heights are over the Rock wedges: Roblox's mesher moves Rock and Ice vertices by a pseudo-random offset that depends on where they are (its slides call it a per-material 'shift'): the same probe recorded at another origin gives other heights, so these rows change whenever the test list (and so each test's origin) changes, and the hash is not known.
- **Swim**: R15 matches throughout (sink, float, speed, jump, back on its feet on the shelf). An R6 that jumps out lands back as Swimming > GettingUp > Running > Freefall > Swimming in Roblox; on the shelf it stays Swimming with FloorMaterial Air, where ours stands up; its jump rises 6.77 against 7.84.
- **DataStoreValues**: A live DataStore is eventually consistent: right after the writes, ListKeysAsync (list_keys, all_scopes_keys) still lists nothing, ListVersionsAsync shows only the newest version and says more pages follow, and GetVersionAsync can't find the version SetAsync just returned (get_version). Ours is consistent at once, as the docs describe it once the writes have propagated.
- **MemoryStore**: queue_waited measures ReadAsync's wait with os.clock, the host's CPU clock; a headless run steps the game faster than real time, so the one-second wait (game time) passes in less. In a live Play (local) the two agree.
- **HttpJson**: num_third_e10: 1e10/3 needs 17 digits to round-trip and Roblox picks 3333333333.3333337 among the 17-digit strings that do, where ours prints the nearest, 3333333333.3333335. Both read back as the same number.
- **Pathfinding**: pass_through_*: the live server gave NoPath for a PathfindingModifier with PassThrough added just before ComputeAsync (its navigation mesh hadn't caught up); Local Run builds its grid per request, so the door is passed at once.
- **SocialPolicy.voice_enabled**: the recording account has voice chat; a local run has no voice, so IsVoiceEnabledForUserIdAsync is false
- **WebMarket.places_first**: the sandbox is published and has a place; an unpublished Local Run (GameId 0) has none, so its first page is empty
- **WebServices.stream_no_url**: WebStreamClient is Studio-only: the live server (RCC) refuses it, a Local Run follows Studio and opens streams
- **ClientApis.key_one**: Sober is the Android client and answers the KeyCode's name; desktop Roblox (the target) gives the character a US layout types for the key, per the GetStringForKeyCode reference, which is what we answer
- **ClientApis.key_semicolon**: Sober is the Android client and answers the KeyCode's name; desktop Roblox (the target) gives the character a US layout types for the key, per the GetStringForKeyCode reference, which is what we answer
- **ClientApis.key_shift**: Sober answers the KeyCode name; what desktop Roblox gives for a key that types no character isn't documented or measured, we answer an empty string
- **AudioApi.bad_pin**: the recording printed a table address; the test now prints the type and length, awaiting a new recording
- **EditableApi.destroyed_read**: the row prints a buffer's memory address, which no two runs share; the part that matters, a read after Destroy still handing back a buffer rather than erroring, matches
- **HumanoidDeath.breaks_hand_moved_15**: Roblox's broken R15 topples slower at first: a hand 0.8 stud away by 15 beats; ours is 2 studs away by then (the head lands as Roblox's by 60). The early push apart is quicker than Roblox's.
- **WorldApi.terrain_write_channels**: Roblox reads a half-solid cell sharing water back as 0.496 (127/256); ours as 0.5. Quantisation detail.
- **WorldApi.translate**: A script-made model's WorldPivot reads as its live bounding-box centre on Roblox; ours keeps the pivot it was carried to (pivot.rs stores one on the first move).
- **PartPhysics.group_restored_y**: Roblox's part, put back above the floor once its group collides again, barely moves in 30 beats (503.2); ours lands and settles at 504.5. Not traced.
- **PartPhysics.impulse_v**: A live server's beat after the push takes 1.5 frames of gravity (vy -4.9); ours takes one (-3.3). The push itself matches.
- **Explosion.moved_D12**: Ten beats after the blast the cubes have hit each other; where each ends up is within 3 studs of Roblox's but not closer.
- **Explosion.moved_D2**: Ten beats after the blast the cubes have hit each other; where each ends up is within 3 studs of Roblox's but not closer.
- **Explosion.moved_D20**: Ten beats after the blast the cubes have hit each other; where each ends up is within 3 studs of Roblox's but not closer.
- **Explosion.moved_D4**: Ten beats after the blast the cubes have hit each other; where each ends up is within 3 studs of Roblox's but not closer.
- **Explosion.moved_D8**: Ten beats after the blast the cubes have hit each other; where each ends up is within 3 studs of Roblox's but not closer.
- **Explosion.v_D20**: A live server's beat after the push takes 1.5 frames of gravity (vy -4.9); ours takes one (-3.3). The push itself matches.
- **Explosion.v_heavy**: A live server's beat after the push takes 1.5 frames of gravity (vy -4.9); ours takes one (-3.3). The push itself matches.
- **Explosion.w_heavy**: Roblox's blast also spins a cube 6 studs off (19 rad/s about Z) though the push is straight along Z; the source of that spin is not found.
- **Explosion.weak_v**: A live server's beat after the push takes 1.5 frames of gravity (vy -4.9); ours takes one (-3.3). The push itself matches.
- **AvatarApi.build**: the sandbox place records with Workspace.AvatarJointUpgrade rolled out, so BuildRigFromAttachments makes AnimationConstraints with BallSocketConstraints and NoCollisionConstraints; ours makes the Motor6Ds a place without the upgrade gets (AnimationConstraint rigs aren't simulated yet, and every other character path here is Motor6D)
- **AvatarApi.build_again**: the sandbox place records with Workspace.AvatarJointUpgrade rolled out, so BuildRigFromAttachments makes AnimationConstraints with BallSocketConstraints and NoCollisionConstraints; ours makes the Motor6Ds a place without the upgrade gets (AnimationConstraint rigs aren't simulated yet, and every other character path here is Motor6D)
- **AvatarApi.build_later**: the sandbox place records with Workspace.AvatarJointUpgrade rolled out, so BuildRigFromAttachments makes AnimationConstraints with BallSocketConstraints and NoCollisionConstraints; ours makes the Motor6Ds a place without the upgrade gets (AnimationConstraint rigs aren't simulated yet, and every other character path here is Motor6D)
- **AvatarApi.replace**: the last entry is the new hand (left at the origin) less the rig's LeftLowerArm, which rests 0.004 stud higher in ours: the R15 standing height, not ReplaceBodyPartR15, whose results all match
